# Langfuse

This skill helps you use Langfuse effectively across all common workflows: instrumenting applications, migrating prompts, debugging traces, and accessing data programmatically.

## Core Principles

Follow these principles for ALL Langfuse work:

1. **Documentation First**: NEVER implement based on memory. Always fetch current docs before writing code (Langfuse updates frequently) See the section below on how to access documentation.
2. **CLI for Data Access**: Use `langfuse-cli` when querying/modifying Langfuse data. See the section below on how to use the CLI. 
3. **Best Practices by Use Case**: Check the relevant reference file below for use-case-specific guidelines before implementing
4. **Use latest Langfuse versions**: Unless the user specified otherwise or there's a good reason, always use the latest version of Langfuse SDKs/APIs.


## Use case specific references

- instrumenting an existing function/application: references/instrumentation.md
- migrating prompts from a codebase into Langfuse: references/prompt-migration.md
- capturing user feedback (thumbs, ratings, implicit signals) as scores on traces: references/user-feedback.md
- further tips on using the Langfuse CLI: references/cli.md
- upgrading or migrating Langfuse SDKs to the latest version: references/sdk-upgrade.md
- submitting feedback about this skill: references/skill-feedback.md

## 1. Langfuse API via CLI

Use the `langfuse-cli` to interact with the full Langfuse REST API from the command line. Run via npx (no install required):

Start by discovering the schema and available arguments:

```bash
# Discover all available resources
npx langfuse-cli api __schema

# List actions for a resource
npx langfuse-cli api <resource> --help

# Show args/options for a specific action
npx langfuse-cli api <resource> <action> --help
```

### Credentials

Set environment variables before making calls:

```bash
export LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-...
export LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...
export LANGFUSE_HOST=https://cloud.langfuse.com # example for EU cloud. For US cloud it's us.cloud.langfuse.com, and can also be a self-hosted URL. The server must always be specified in order to access Langfuse.
```

If not set, ask the user for their API keys (found in Langfuse UI → Settings → API Keys).

### Detailed CLI Reference

For common workflows, tips, and full usage patterns, see [references/cli.md](references/cli.md).

## 2. Langfuse Documentation

Three methods to access Langfuse docs, in order of preference. **Always prefer your application's native web fetch and search tools** (e.g., `WebFetch`, `WebSearch`, `mcp_fetch`, etc.) over `curl` when available. The URLs and patterns below work with any fetching method — the `curl` examples are just illustrative.

### 2a. Documentation Index (llms.txt)

Fetch the full index of all documentation pages:

```bash
curl -s https://langfuse.com/llms.txt
```

Returns a structured list of every doc page with titles and URLs. Use this to discover the right page for a topic, then fetch that page directly.

Alternatively, you can start on `https://langfuse.com/docs` and explore the site to find the page you need.

### 2b. Fetch Individual Pages as Markdown

Any page listed in llms.txt can be fetched as markdown by appending `.md` to its path or by using `Accept: text/markdown` in the request headers. Use this when you know which page contains the information needed. Returns clean markdown with code examples and configuration details.

```bash
curl -s "https://langfuse.com/docs/observability/overview.md"
curl -s "https://langfuse.com/docs/observability/overview" -H "Accept: text/markdown"
```

### 2c. Search Documentation

When you need to find information across all docs and github issues/discussions without knowing the specific page:

```bash
curl -s "https://langfuse.com/api/search-docs?query=<url-encoded-query>"
```

Example:

```bash
curl -s "https://langfuse.com/api/search-docs?query=How+do+I+trace+LangGraph+agents"
```

Returns a JSON response with:

- `query`: the original query
- `answer`: a JSON string containing an array of matching documents, each with:
  - `url`: link to the doc page
  - `title`: page title
  - `source.content`: array of relevant text excerpts from the page

Search is a great fallback if you cannot find the relevant pages or need more context. Especially useful when debugging issues as all GitHub Issues and Discussions are also indexed. Responses can be large — extract only the relevant portions.

### Documentation Workflow

1. Start with **llms.txt** to orient — scan for relevant page titles
2. **Fetch specific pages** when you identify the right one
3. Fall back to **search** when the topic is unclear and you want more context

## Skill Feedback

When the user expresses that something about this skill is not working as expected, gives incorrect guidance, is missing information, or could be improved — offer to submit feedback to the Langfuse skill maintainers. This includes when:

- The skill gave wrong or outdated instructions
- A workflow didn't produce the expected result
- The user wishes the skill covered something it doesn't
- The user explicitly says something like "this should work differently" or "this is wrong"

**Do NOT trigger this** for issues with Langfuse itself (the product) — only for issues with this skill's instructions and behavior.

When triggered, follow the process in [references/skill-feedback.md](references/skill-feedback.md).

# Reference: TIER-1 OFFICIAL Anthropic SDK source-level cite-upgrade `Z:/repos/deps/claude-agent-sdk-python/src/claude_agent_sdk/client.py:312-564 @ HEAD b512f256450dba8f0dd1399e485563b7deb9c534` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02 via Ship 38 Marker Decay refresh; client.py UNCHANGED in 8348d1f8..b512f256 span per Ship 38 diff probe; Wave 3 source-mining originally at 8348d1f8 (absorbed via Ship 31 commit `fea597b` G-8 source-mining)] — `ClaudeSDKClient` runtime-control + introspection surface, 8 G-8 rows / 10 SDK methods: `interrupt()` / `set_permission_mode(mode)` / `set_model(model)` / `rewind_files(user_message_id)` / `reconnect_mcp_server(server_name)` / `toggle_mcp_server(server_name, enabled)` / `stop_task(task_id)` / `get_mcp_status()` + `get_context_usage()` + `get_server_info()`. T1-T7 lifecycle composes ON TOP OF these runtime-control primitives — sss currently mutates via restart-with-flag (e.g. `--permission-mode`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL=`, `disabledMcpjsonServers` settings) instead of programmatic `set_*()` calls; the gap is "felt as friction" (e.g. dreamweaverhoudini 89% session-cap event 2026-04-30 — programmatic `set_model` to fallback could have been gradient runtime instead of restart). STUDY-PILOT for if/when sss launcher pivots to ClaudeSDKClient lifecycle from CLI-only.

# Source: this rule is design-novel for claude-sota (no Z:/claude port source). TIER-3 local evidence trails synthesize the empirical T1-T7 lifecycle (T7 ask-without-act gate added 2026-04-27 per `feedback_auto_deep_dive_no_pause.md`; T4 wording corrected from "BEFORE remote push" to "post-push cumulative" 2026-04-27 per self-audit fire — wire was always PostToolUse, label was drift). Original T1-T6 lifecycle operated across iter-2 through iter-8 rule-port sequence.

# Dogfood failure: v0.1 of this rule was drafted BEFORE T1 — exactly the violation the rule codifies. codex-t1-gate WARN fired correctly. Retroactive T1 fired post-draft (verdict at .claude/state/codex_consult_cross_model_consensus_design_OUT.txt — NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88, 5 findings: 2 high + 3 medium). v0.2 (this version) integrates the fix-forward in a single iter per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md NEEDS-REVISION disposition. n=2 evidence for the T1-mandatory rule (n=1 was iter-1 sota-loop-discipline.md v0.1 same dogfood pattern, deferred to reports/ at REJECT). The recovery path here is fix-forward-before-first-commit, NOT REVERT-AND-REMOVE — the file was never committed without T1 verdict on file.

Single-model design produces silent drift. Opus 4.7 drafting a rule looks coherent to Opus reading it back; codex GPT-5.5 reading the same rule independently catches mismatches Opus's self-audit misses (cite line-range errors, terminology drift across sibling rules, internal-vs-external state conflation, narrative-vs-protocol confusion). The cross-model consensus contract is: **Opus 4.7 designs + drafts → GPT-5.5 codex audits at lifecycle touchpoints (T1-T7) → verdict drives ship / fix-forward / STOP**. No commit lands on a design surface without codex's verdict on file.

This rule codifies the contract. It interlocks with `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` (verdict trajectory disposition), `synthesis-layer-verify.md` (verdict report shape), `evidence-policy.md` (verdict marker discipline), and `audit-action-loop.md` (post-audit drift closure).

### Discipline (Pattern A from real GPT-5.5 codex T1 fire-28)

- A SECTION that introduces or modifies a normative mechanism MUST add a row to this Map.
- A SECTION that only inherits / cross-refs / lists anti-patterns MAY omit (no new policy = no new authority claim).
- COMPOSED claims MUST follow the mechanical source-class reduction lattice at `.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md` rule #8 (UPGRADED at Wave 17 fire-36 per real GPT-5.5 codex T1 fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable"). Concretely: declare `constituents=[<class1@cite1>, ...]` + `effective_tier=MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Authority Map rows above DECLARE constituents (per row) + EFFECTIVE class is computed per the lattice — this section does NOT carry a softer duplicate of the reduction rule.
- This Map is the BODY-level cite-trail discipline that complements frontmatter TIER-1 anchors at lines 9-11.

## The contract (cardinal rule)

For every Edit / Write / MultiEdit on a design surface (gate is default-in-scope and skips only `_UNIVERSAL_EXCLUSIONS` per `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:23-28,104-108,232,1717-1719`; common in-scope: rules, agents, commands, skills, settings, `.claude/schemas/*.json`, CLAUDE*.md, .mcp.json, PINS.json — NOT exhaustive):

1. **Opus drafts**. Synthesize from primary sources (Z:/repos/deps file:line, official docs, prior in-repo rules). Write the consult prompt that frames the design.
2. **Codex audits at T1**. Fire `codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review-exec --output-last-message .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt < .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt` BEFORE writing the design surface (or at minimum, in parallel — but with the file unstaged until verdict integrates). **Canonical pair-basename rule**: prompt path is `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt`; verdict path is `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`. The codex_t1_consult_gate `_consult_pairs()` algorithm strips `_OUT` from output basename and looks for prompt at exact stem — `codex_consult_<topic>_PROMPT.txt` does NOT pair with `codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`. Operator-side fix for FM-08 codex-t1-gate WARN false-positive class (n=14+ session arc 2026-04-29→05-02 caused by my prompts living in `tmp/` instead of `.claude/state/<topic>.txt`).
3. **Verdict integrates**. Apply first-round dispositions from `§Verdict shapes + closed-loop integration`: APPROVE → ship; NEEDS-REVISION → apply prescribed edits as Pattern A fix-forward per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` (max 1 fix-forward iter per round) and re-fire T1 on the patched design surface; REJECT → STOP, report, and do not commit (remove untracked file before commit).
4. **Verdict reading: EOF FIRST**. Per `feedback_codex_t1_verdict_reading_discipline.md` (TIER-3 local provenance) — codex deep-review-exec may echo verdict templates near the start and emit the real verdict at EOF; `wc -l <file>` first; `grep -nE "VERDICT:|^F-[0-9]|conf=|confidence|APPROVE|NEEDS-REVISION|REJECT|severity (P[0-3])" <file> | tail -20`; then `tail -200 <file>` to confirm the final verdict block at EOF.
5. **Codex audits at T2/T3 (when wired)**. T2 reviews the working tree before commit; T3 fires automatically post-commit.
6. **Ship lands** with the codex verdict file referenced in commit body (`.claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt` for T1; `.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_<sha>.txt` for T3 when wired).

The contract is non-negotiable. Recovery paths by violation timing:

- **Pre-commit T1 miss** (file untracked, no commit yet — e.g. v0.1 of this rule): retroactive T1 + single fix-forward if verdict permits. NEEDS-REVISION → fix-forward; APPROVE → ship; REJECT → STOP and remove the file before any commit.
- **Post-commit single-model design surface** (commit landed without T1 verdict on file): per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` §Disposition signal severity gate — Outcome B REVERT-AND-REMOVE on unresolved high/critical, Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE on medium-with-strong-mitigation.

The first path (pre-commit miss) is the recovery path used by this very rule: v0.1 was untracked when the dogfood failure surfaced, retroactive T1 ran, NEEDS-REVISION verdict integrates here in v0.2 — no commit ever landed without T1 verdict on file.

**Discipline-side / hook-side relationship**: PreToolUse `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:232,1717-1719` targets Edit/Write/MultiEdit; when T1 is absent, the default path warns (non-blocking), while `CODEX_T1_GATE_STRICT=1` with auto-spawn enabled routes through AUTO-T1 at `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:1847-1868` and `_auto_spawn_codex_t1` at `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:1238-1455`. This `§The contract` body is the DISCIPLINE-side complement to hook-side enforcement — agent body sections point HERE rather than duplicate mechanics. cf. this file's Dogfood failure note at `cross-model-consensus.md:14` where the rule itself was caught by the gate it codifies before first commit.

## Profile selection rule

Codex profile presets live at `Z:/claude-sota/.codex/config.toml` (the local repo-resident `.codex/` is the authoritative config; the parent cache path `Z:/claude/.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex/1.0.4/` is TIER-3 operational provenance only). Three canonical profiles cover the T1-T7 lifecycle (table updated iter-93 2026-05-02 per codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.91 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_iter93_t2_exec_profile_addition_OUT.txt` Q4 prescription):

- **`deep-review-exec`**: cloud `gpt-5.5` xhigh-effort + danger-full-access sandbox + service_tier=fast + verbosity=high. Used by T1 pre-edit consult hook (`codex_t1_consult_gate.py`), T3 postcommit hook (`codex_postcommit_review.py:599` per codex T1 verdict at `codex_consult_iter93_*_OUT.txt` Q6 P3), T4 post-push hook (`codex_prepush_review.py:593`), and T6 stop-gate (`codex_stop_review_gate.py`). Token budget ~5K-10K per invocation.
- **`deep-review`**: cloud `gpt-5.5` xhigh-effort + read-only sandbox + service_tier=fast + verbosity=high. Used by T5 manual `/plan-codex-review` slash command (read-only suffices for prompt-context bounded reads). Token budget ~5K-10K per invocation.
- **`t2-exec`** (added iter-93 2026-05-02): cloud `gpt-5.5` HIGH-effort (NOT xhigh — T2 mechanical scope) + read-only sandbox + service_tier=fast + verbosity=medium. Used for **operator-side custom-prompt T2 reviews** ONLY: `codex exec --ephemeral -p t2-exec < .claude/state/codex_t2_<topic>.txt > .claude/state/codex_t2_<topic>_OUT.txt 2>&1`. Token budget ~1K-3K per invocation.

**Important asymmetry — T2 commit-time runtime hook does NOT use `-p`**: the runtime pre-commit gate `codex_t2_pre_commit_gate.py:424` uses `codex exec review --uncommitted` which REJECTS `-p` per `codex-cli-flag-positioning.md` matrix row 1. The hook routes via codex CLI's default profile. The `t2-exec` profile is exclusively for operator-side standalone T2 fires with custom prompts (e.g., this rule's own iter-93 retroactive T1 + sister-update). Per codex T1 Q4 prescription verbatim: "Do not codify `t2-exec` as a hook T2 profile: `codex exec review --uncommitted` rejects `-p`, so add `t2-exec` as an operator-side custom-prompt T2 convention only".

**Legacy / non-T1-T7 profiles** (kept in config.toml for batch / classification workloads, NOT for verdict-composition T2):
- **`headless-exec`** (gemma4:vision local Ollama): RETIRED for operator-side T2 custom-prompt reviews per n=2 same-arc 180s timeouts at iter-92 fires `bfawoapn0` + `bl47e7iin` (commit `f3c1e57` body Pattern B trail). Local 7B-class VLM cannot compose verdict block in 180s budget for natural-language T2 prompts. Still correct for pure classification / image / multimodal tasks where Ollama VLM excels. Memory: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/feedback_headless_exec_t2_profile_drift_2026_05_02.md`.
- **`local`** / **`review`** / **`strict`**: see `.codex/config.toml:[profiles.X]` blocks for use cases.

Pick by surface complexity and depth-of-reasoning needed, not by file count. A 21-line evidence-policy port can need `deep-review-exec` if the cite-class adjudication is non-trivial; a 500-line working-tree diff can use `t2-exec` if the changes are mechanical and need cloud-fast-tier verdict latency.

## Timeout + failover policy

- T1 pre-edit consult: **240s narrow** (3-axis prompt) / **900s deep** (full design audit). Narrow is default; escalate to deep only when the design touches multiple sibling rules or introduces a new cite-class.
- T2/T3/T4: **120-180s** sufficient for `--uncommitted` or postcommit-diff scope.
- T5 plan-stage: **240s**.
- T6 stop-gate: **900s** (covers full session-end review).

**On timeout**: fail-closed → mine the partial trace for embedded evidence, document the timeout in the commit body (`T1 INCONCLUSIVE-TIMEOUT after 240s — partial trace mined, no NEW-CLASS findings emerged`), proceed only if no high-severity findings surfaced from the partial output. `feedback_codex_t1_timeout_extract_partial_evidence.md` is TIER-3 local pattern provenance (FORWARD-REF — pending memory-port to claude-sota).

**On codex unavailable** (`codex exec` returns cmd-not-found / 3 consecutive failures): the documented `local-judge` MCP fallback is currently UNAVAILABLE — `local-judge` is in `.claude/settings.json:disabledMcpjsonServers` as of 2026-05-03, so `mcp__local-judge__route` + `invoke_judge` will return tool-not-found. Recovery options:

- **(a) DEFAULT — Queue + retry**: queue T1 unavailable + codex re-review when codex CLI recovers (preserves cross-model consensus invariant; preferred for non-urgent design reviews). **Maximum queue window** (per Wave 48 Agent A finding F-5 conf=0.80): 4h for medium-risk ships; 24h for low-risk ships. If codex unavailable beyond window: high-risk ships BLOCK until recovery; medium-risk evaluate option (b) with fresh smoke-probe AND explicit `[OVERRIDE]` marker; low-risk proceed with Pattern B HNF disposition per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md`.
- **(b) FALLBACK — Direct Ollama HTTP bridge** at `http://127.0.0.1:11700/api/generate` against `qwen3.6:judge` model. Backend liveness MEASURED [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via curl `/api/version` returning `{"version":"0.22.1"}` + codex T1 secondary probe `/api/tags` listing `qwen3.6:judge` + `gemma4:vision`].

> **⚠️ CAUTION — Option (b) `/api/generate` endpoint is UNCERTIFIED.** Neither the `/api/generate` endpoint nor the `qwen3.6:judge` model's verdict-shape compatibility has been smoke-tested for cross-model T1 verdict-composition. **First-use of option (b) MUST probe before trusting verdicts**: send a minimal payload with `"stream": false` set explicitly (e.g., `{"model": "qwen3.6:judge", "prompt": "APPROVE, NEEDS-REVISION, or REJECT?", "stream": false}` — without `"stream": false`, Ollama `/api/generate` defaults to NDJSON streaming and the JSON-shape parse below will false-negative even when endpoint is healthy) + verify `{"response": "..."}` JSON shape returns within 60s + verify model emits APPROVE / NEEDS-REVISION / REJECT structured output as expected by `cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict shapes`. If smoke-probe fails, fall back to option (a) deferred-queue rather than trust an uncertified verdict.

Mark commit body — option (a): `T1 via option-a-deferred — codex re-review queued`; option (b) **DOWNGRADED-MODE** (per Wave 48 Agent A finding F-2 P1 conf=0.88): `T1 DOWNGRADED: qwen3.6:judge via Ollama (UNCERTIFIED — cross-model gate NOT satisfied; GPT-5.5 unavailable); ship under Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md with explicit [OVERRIDE] marker; codex re-review queued when GPT-5.5 available`. Note: option (b) is renamed FALLBACK → **DOWNGRADED-MODE** to signal trust-boundary change at label level.

## Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate (Wave 17 fire-24 codification per E2 audit P1-A)

# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota/CLAUDE.local.md` ENV block (f) — `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-6` (Anthropic Max Opus depletion fallback, deployed 2026-04-29)
# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota/tmp/wave17-E2-arc-level-review-2026-05-04.md §5` (P1-A finding: A3+C2+B2+D2+E2 ran as Sonnet stand-ins; true GPT-5.5 penetration ~25% not asserted 75%)
# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/named-failure-modes.md` FM-17 row (subagent fleet-depletion final-return-loss; pool-funneling parent class)
# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/agents/gpt5-reviewer.md` frontmatter — fail-closed contract: "Codex unavailable is never a SKIP condition; invoke this agent and return VERDICT: BLOCK"

When `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` is set to a model OTHER than the canonical gpt5-reviewer / gpt5-archaeologist target (currently `gpt-5.5` via codex CLI), the subagent runs as a **same-model stand-in** rather than a true cross-model voice. The cross-model consensus invariant — that codex GPT-5.5 catches what Opus/Sonnet self-audit miss — does NOT structurally hold under stand-in dispatch. The dispatch surface looks identical to the orchestrator (same agent name, same prompt template, same return shape) but the model-asymmetry that makes the gate meaningful is silently absent.

### The mandate

A `gpt5-reviewer` / `gpt5-archaeologist` dispatch under env-funneling MUST do ONE of:

1. **(Default)** Return `VERDICT: BLOCK` per the agent's fail-closed contract at `.claude/agents/gpt5-reviewer.md` frontmatter. Stand-in dispatch does NOT satisfy the cross-model gate; BLOCK is the safe action under structural same-model degradation.
2. **(Explicit-disclosure)** Return APPROVE / NEEDS-REVISION / REJECT WITH explicit `STAND-IN-NOTICE: agent ran under CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=<model> stand-in per CLAUDE.local.md ENV block (f); cross-model gate NOT structurally satisfied for this dispatch` in the verdict body's first 5 lines. The orchestrator then bears the integration decision: re-fire via real codex CLI, accept stand-in verdict with documented gate-bypass rationale, or REVERT-AND-REMOVE per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` Outcome B.
3. **(Operator escalation)** If codex CLI is unreachable (FM-03 D1 transport): return BLOCK + queue-and-retry per the existing "On codex unavailable" recovery options at §"Timeout + failover policy" above.

### Orchestrator integration discipline

When integrating a STAND-IN-NOTICE verdict into a composite synthesis (e.g., Wave fan-out close-synthesis.md), the orchestrator MUST:

1. **Surface the stand-in penetration rate** in the close-synthesis verdict body — e.g., "5/9 dispatches ran as Sonnet stand-in (~56%); cross-model coverage is ~44% of nominal; treat composite verdict accordingly".
2. **Reject blanket "N% GPT-5.5 dispatched" claims** without per-dispatch classification (true GPT-5.5 via `codex exec` CLI vs Sonnet stand-in via Anthropic SDK vs codex-rescue wrapper-around-codex-CLI). This was the empirical class-error caught in Wave 17 E2 review §5 — claimed 75% GPT-5.5 vs measured ~25%.
3. **Cite the stand-in classification** in commit body when the ship integrates a stand-in verdict (e.g., `T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.83 [STAND-IN per CLAUDE.local.md ENV (f); see tmp/wave17-E2-arc-level-review-2026-05-04.md §5]`). Per `evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay corollary + the MUST-USE `[VERIFIED via <path>]` extension at §"Evidence marker discipline" above.

### Empirical evidence (n=5 same-arc Wave 17 2026-05-04)

| # | Wave | Agent | Self-declared stand-in evidence | Cross-model gate satisfied? |
|---|------|-------|--------------------------------|------------------------------|
| 1 | Wave 17 fire-16 A3 | gpt5-reviewer | YES — `tmp/wave17-A3-adversarial-review-2026-05-04.md:4` "agent: gpt5-reviewer (claude-sonnet-4-6 stand-in per FM-17 pool funneling)" | NO |
| 2 | Wave 17 fire-16 C2 | gpt5-reviewer | YES — `tmp/wave17-C2-adversarial-fire16-cascade-2026-05-04.md:5` "agent: gpt5-reviewer (claude-sonnet-4-6 proxy — codex CLI unavailable; fail-closed per agent SYSTEM PROMPT contract)" | NO |
| 3 | Wave 17 fire-20 B2 | gpt5-archaeologist | INFERRED — FM-19 truncated, but class-wrapper inherits same env-funneling | NO (assumed stand-in by class inheritance) |
| 4 | Wave 17 fire-20 D2 | gpt5-archaeologist | INFERRED — FM-19 truncated, but class-wrapper inherits same env-funneling | NO (assumed stand-in by class inheritance) |
| 5 | Wave 17 E2 audit | gpt5-reviewer | YES — `tmp/wave17-E2-arc-level-review-2026-05-04.md:4` "agent: gpt5-reviewer (claude-sonnet-4-6 proxy — codex CLI unavailable per readonly Bash guard FM-19)" | NO |

True GPT-5.5 penetration ~25% (5 codex CLI jobs out of ~20 total dispatches per E2 §5 fleet dispatch classification) — NOT 75% as previously claimed. The user directive "USE GPT5.5 AGGRESSIVELY" has NOT been operationally satisfied at the gpt5-* subagent layer; it IS satisfied at the codex CLI layer when foreground+tee fires successfully.

### Recovery actions for orchestrator

Per the n=5 evidence ladder, when the orchestrator detects stand-in pattern:

- **Compose**: orchestrator-side prompt to gpt5-reviewer/gpt5-archaeologist MUST include `IF YOUR FRONTMATTER MODEL ≠ gpt-5.5 OR YOUR DISPATCH RUNS UNDER CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=<not-gpt-5.5>: return VERDICT: BLOCK + STAND-IN-NOTICE per cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate.` This forces explicit disclosure rather than relying on agent self-awareness.
- **Audit**: at iter-close synthesis, orchestrator MUST classify each gpt5-* dispatch into {true-GPT-5.5-via-codex-CLI / Sonnet-stand-in-via-SDK / codex-rescue-wrapper-of-codex-CLI / unknown-FM-19-truncated} and surface penetration rate.
- **Remediate**: when penetration <50%, recommend operator-side `unset CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` or set `=gpt-5.5` (where SDK supports it) before re-firing the wave.

### Update triggers

Re-evaluate this section when:
- `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` env is unset or set to a model OTHER than `claude-sonnet-4-6` (Anthropic Max Opus pool restored OR pool-funnel target changes) — flip mandate from MUST-BLOCK-by-default to "warn if env-funneled" since stand-in becomes exceptional rather than default
- Anthropic CC ships a per-dispatch model attestation primitive (e.g., `agent_dispatch_actual_model` hook field, `subagent_provenance` system-reminder) that surfaces stand-in status structurally rather than via agent self-declaration
- A 6th cross-arc instance surfaces (would expand the n=5 evidence ladder; promotion-ladder for sub-rule extraction at n=6+)
- A subagent class ships that has NO equivalent codex CLI tool path (would require expanding the discipline beyond gpt5-reviewer / gpt5-archaeologist to include the new class)

## Verdict shapes + closed-loop integration

Codex T1 verdicts come in 3 shapes. **First-round T1 verdict disposition is distinct from closed-loop arc disposition** — closed-loop outcomes (A/B/C) only invoke when the SAME logical surface re-fires across rounds:

| Verdict | First-round disposition | Re-fire (closed-loop arc) |
|---|---|---|
| **APPROVE** | Ship as-designed (commit lands with verdict file referenced) | n/a — first-round APPROVE never invokes closed-loop |
| **NEEDS-REVISION** | Apply findings, single fix-forward (max 1 per iter per `.claude/rules/sota-loop-discipline.md` §G5 — pending P2.h port). Re-fire codex T1 on the patched file | If re-fire NEEDS-REVISION → Outcome A ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (cap-exit at iter-5 per §closed-loop §G5) |
| **REJECT** | STOP, do not commit; if file is untracked remove it; escalate | If re-fire still REJECT → Outcome B REVERT-AND-REMOVE (per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` §Disposition signal severity-gate hard-deny on unresolved high/critical) |

Confidence trajectory is the load-bearing signal across re-fires. Track across rounds, not just final round. Manual override (Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE) requires explicit `[OVERRIDE]` marker per `evidence-policy.md` and applies only to medium-severity findings with strong mitigation documented in commit body.

## Severity taxonomy mapping (codex P0-P3 ↔ industry Critical/Important/Minor)

# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/superpowers/skills/requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md:64-93 @ HEAD e7a2d16476bf042e9add4699c9d018a90f86e4a6 [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 via wave-3 source-mining methodology-audit-PASS at docs/sota-grand-catalog/wave-3-2026-04-30/source-mining/superpowers-deep-read.md (commit 11bf5eb)]

Per superpowers (named-T2 author obra; 3-axis convergence-gate PASS per wave-3 source-mining), industry-standard code-review severity vocabulary maps to sss codex P0/P1/P2/P3 numeric scale. Use this mapping in T1/T2/T3 verdict reports for cross-tool portability:

| Codex severity | Industry vocab | Action threshold | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0** (CRITICAL) | **Critical** | STOP; do not commit; revert if landed | Security vulnerability; data loss; default-allow on destructive Bash; secret leak |
| **P1** (HIGH) | **Important** | Block commit; require fix-forward before merge | Logic bug in load-bearing path; broken contract; missing test for DENY-emitting hook |
| **P2** (MEDIUM) | **Minor (high)** | Apply fix-forward in same PR; not blocking | Suboptimal pattern; dead code; marker discipline gap; cite-tier downgrade |
| **P3** (LOW) | **Minor (low)** | Queue as separate ship; document in commit body | Stylistic; naming consistency; doc-only nits |

Source convention: superpowers `code-reviewer.md:64-93` uses Critical / Important / Minor as the 3-tier external-facing taxonomy; sss codex uses P0/P1/P2/P3 4-tier internal numeric scale. This mapping bridges the two so cross-tool review reports (e.g., when codex T2 verdict is consumed by external reviewers using superpowers-style rubric, or when sss reviews a PR authored against superpowers conventions) maintain semantic equivalence.

Cross-references:
- `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Disposition signal severity-gate` — hard-deny triggers on unresolved Critical/Important (P0/P1) findings
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — orthogonal axis (severity classifies findings; subclaim-type classifies claim sources)
- `evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay — severity is also marker-decayable (P1 yesterday may be P0 today if upstream context changed)

## Risk-stratified verification gating

# Reference: `https://resources.anthropic.com/2026-agentic-coding-trends-report` (TIER-1 Anthropic; full PDF at `https://resources.anthropic.com/hubfs/2026%20Agentic%20Coding%20Trends%20Report.pdf`) [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via Wave-6 Agent M perplexity_research [17][31][48]] — Anthropic 2026 Agentic Coding Trends Report identifies risk-stratified gating as the SOTA pattern for AI-agent code change verification: low-risk = full-agent-delegate + post-commit review; medium-risk = pre-commit human review (agent implements, human approves); high-risk = design review BEFORE agent implements.
# Reference: Wave-6 Agent M Round-4 task design at `tmp/wave6-agent-m-sota-git-practice-2026-05-03.md:165-196` (ADOPT-NOW conf=0.92; cohort attribution C5 named-author Anthropic; Axis 1+2+3 PASS via STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS predicate satisfied — Anthropic-org-as-named-T2-equivalent maintainer).

Beyond the **finding-severity** taxonomy at §"Severity taxonomy mapping" above (which classifies what codex found AFTER work landed), the **task risk-class** axis classifies the change BEFORE work begins. Both axes are orthogonal — severity describes findings (P0/P1/P2/P3); risk-class describes tasks (low/medium/high). Both inform the T0-T7 lifecycle gate selection.

### The 3-tier risk taxonomy + T0-T7 gate mapping

| Risk class | Task signature | Verification gate | sss T0-T7 mapping |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Low-risk** | Reversible operations on isolated surfaces (tests / docs / non-cardinal-rule edits / bounded scripts ≤200 LOC where convergence is well-understood); single-axis-change with clear primary-source backing | Full-agent-delegate + **post-commit review only** (T3 PostToolUse `Bash(git commit *)`) | T1 SKIP per §"Mechanical-mirror exception" at `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` IF predicates met; else light T1 + T3 verify |
| **Medium-risk** | Multi-file ships / new mechanics / behavioral changes / existing-surface edits with potential ripple; rule-layer or skill-layer additions ≤200 LOC | Pre-commit cross-model review (**T2 commit-time hook** at `Bash(git commit *)` PreToolUse — agent implements, codex GPT-5.5 reviews uncommitted tree before commit lands) | T1 pre-edit + T2 working-tree + T3 post-commit (full lifecycle) |

# Audit-action-loop discipline

# TIER-3 port provenance only: ported from Z:/claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md HEAD. Parent CCC keeps the cycle-N evidence trail; it is not design authority for claude-sota.

A mechanized audit hook (e.g. `claude_md_count_audit.py`, `cite_drift_audit.py`, `mcp_self_audit.py`, `sota_freshness_sweep.py`) fires automatically on every relevant commit, writes drift records to JSONL, and surfaces drift signals to disk. **The audit alone provides 0 protection** — only the audit-action-loop (audit fires → drift surfaces → bounded ship closes) protects against silent staleness.

## Pattern signature

- Stage 1 — **Wire**: audit hook configured to fire on a commit/event matcher (typically `Bash(git commit *)` PostToolUse, or SessionEnd, or an external cadence).
- Stage 2 — **Surface**: audit JSONL accumulates drift records (`any_drift: true`) without consumer action.
- Stage 3 — **Close**: a /loop fire ships a surgical edit that updates the drifted axis with provenance (SHA + cycle attribution).
- Stage 4 — **Re-fire**: the next audit run produces `any_drift: false` for that axis, confirming closure.

## Hook telemetry contract — agent_id/agent_type propagation

**Reference**: `Z:/repos/deps/claude-agent-sdk-python/src/claude_agent_sdk/types.py:246-262,265,274,284,344 @ HEAD b512f256450dba8f0dd1399e485563b7deb9c534 [VERIFIED 2026-05-02 via Ship 38 Marker Decay refresh; Wave 9 audit at agent af32addc used 8348d1f8 — cited line ranges UNCHANGED in 8348d1f8..b512f256 16-commit span per Ship 38 diff probe (types.py +8 SandboxNetworkConfig at L783-806; subprocess_cli.py 22-mod stderr-reader; client.py + __init__.py UNCHANGED)]` — TIER-1 OFFICIAL Anthropic SDK `_SubagentContextMixin` TypedDict mandates `agent_id: str` + `agent_type: str` on the **four tool-lifecycle hook inputs** (`PreToolUseHookInput` `:265`, `PostToolUseHookInput` `:274`, `PostToolUseFailureHookInput` `:284`, `PermissionRequestHookInput` `:344` — all inherit the mixin). Verbatim source comment at `:252`: "When multiple sub-agents run in parallel their tool-lifecycle hooks interleave over the same control channel — this is the only reliable way to attribute each one to the correct sub-agent."

### The contract (forward-only invariant)

Every JSONL-emitting hook in `.claude/hooks/scripts/*.py` MUST persist these two fields when present in hook input:

```json
{
  "ts": "2026-04-30T...",
  "agent_id": "<uuid-or-null-if-main-agent>",
  "agent_type": "<sota-researcher | code-reviewer | codex-rescue | ... | null>",
  "tool_name": "...",
  ...
}
```

**Hook stdin JSON should be read at TOP LEVEL**: `payload.get("agent_id")` and `payload.get("agent_type")`. The SDK comment indicates the CLI derives those values from an internal tool-use context, but hook scripts should NOT expect a nested `tool_use_context` object unless live runtime capture proves one exists (per codex T1 F-002 fix-forward 2026-04-30 — earlier orchestrator paraphrase of `tool_use_context.agent_id` was incorrect).

### Why agent_id is load-bearing

When sss runs 3-6 parallel agents per `.claude/rules/parallel-agent-wave.md`, all of their PreToolUse/PostToolUse/PostToolUseFailure/PermissionRequest hooks fire on the same control channel. Without `agent_id`, JSONL records interleave anonymously:

```
{"ts":"...", "tool_name":"Edit", "file_path":"X.md"}    ← agent A or agent B?
{"ts":"...", "tool_name":"Edit", "file_path":"Y.md"}    ← agent A or agent B?
```

§Stage 3 Close discipline above requires attributing drift to a specific agent for surgical fix-forward. Without `agent_id`, fix-forward becomes guesswork.

### Compliance status (sss spot-check 2026-04-30)

Spot-check via `rg -l "\bagent_id\b" .claude/hooks/scripts -g "*.py"` per codex T1 F-001 fix-forward (verified counts):

- **Canonical-field compliant**: `subagent_start_observer.py`, `worktree_lifecycle_audit.py:91` — both persist `agent_id` and `agent_type` per the SDK contract
- **Aliasing/partial**: `subagent_stop_telemetry.py` — reads SDK fields but aliases them to `subagent_session_id`/`subagent_type`
- **DELETED at Ship #13 commit `54dc8a5` 2026-05-01**: `per_agent_observer.py` was historically non-compliant + unwired (wrote only `ts`/`agent_name`/`event`/`tool`/`duration_ms`); after-the-fact analysis at `reports/sota-e2e-audit-2026-04-27-5agent.md:104` confirmed DEAD CODE; deleted in cleanup-wave per `karpathy-adapted.md §3` discipline. Historical compliance-status retained in this file's Codex T1 Provenance bullet below — DO NOT rewrite per `port-note-discipline.md §6` "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies."

Repo total per spot-check: 53 Python files under `.claude/hooks/scripts`, 48 non-underscore entrypoint-like files, 42 distinct Python scripts referenced by `settings.json`. Full hook-by-hook retrofit/count audit is **queued, NOT part of this ship** — this codifies a forward-only invariant for new hook authors. Earlier orchestrator-side claim "2 of 51 hooks compliant" was OVER (n=7 OVER-claim caught by codex T1 this session); the verified spot-check above replaces it.

## How to apply

1. **Detect drift** — probe `.claude/state/*.jsonl` for `any_drift: true` records that haven't been closed. Useful: `grep -l '"any_drift": true' .claude/state/*.jsonl | head` plus `tail -1 <file>` for latest state.

2. **Pick highest-leverage drift** — per `.claude/rules/codification-threshold.md` ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE, drifts on root-level claims (CLAUDE.md) are higher-leverage than descendant claims (component CLAUDE.md files in `.claude/rules/`, `.claude/commands/`, etc.). Within a tier, prefer the most-cited claim.

3. **Surgical edit** — apply the smallest diff that updates the count + adds a one-clause provenance line citing the SHA + cycle that introduced the drift. Use clause-prefixing pattern: new claim first, prior snapshot preserved as `Prior cycle-N snapshot:` sub-entry.

4. **Atomic batch** — bundle related edits (root + MEMORY.md close entry) in a single `git add ... && git commit` invocation to minimize cross-session bundling risk. Specifically: parallel sessions can commit during the gap between `git add` and `git commit`, and bundle your staged edit into their unrelated commit. The single-invocation form prevents this race.

5. **Verify next audit fires `any_drift: false`** for the closed axis — if not, the fix didn't take. Codex postcommit review on the closing commit IS the verification gate.

## Why

Audit-failure is the worst defect class: a broken audit looks like it's working (entries appear in JSONL) while providing 0 protection. Worse: a working audit that surfaces drift but isn't actioned has the same effect — the mechanization runs, the data accumulates, but nothing closes the drift. Within 12-24h of accumulation, drift becomes "background noise" that humans/agents stop checking. The audit-action-loop is the consumer-side discipline that makes wired audits valuable.

## When NOT to apply

- **Single-fire saturation** — if 4+ drifts surface in one cycle, picking ALL of them violates ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE. Pick highest-leverage; defer rest.
- **Audit script itself is broken** — if JSONL records are empty (e.g. `{"ts":"..."}` only) or use wrong field names, fix the audit before closing drifts based on its output.
- **Drift is intentional** — sometimes a count claim is stale because it's awaiting a related ship. Don't close pre-emptively if the related ship is imminent (next 1-2 fires).
- **Cross-session ownership unclear** — if working tree shows uncommitted changes from a parallel session, prefer `git stash` + commit + restore over editing in-place; minimizes bundling risk.

## Related

- `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` VERIFY corollary 2 (Audit producer-consumer coupling) — claude-sota-local invariant for this rule. Research-protocol's corollary 2 says "every state-correctness audit needs BOTH a write-side trigger wire AND a read-side consumer that surfaces regressions to a decision surface". This rule (`audit-action-loop.md`) IS the consumer-side closure discipline that satisfies that invariant.
- `.claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` — sister rule for the iteration-loop-during-edit moment; this rule covers the post-audit drift-closure moment.
- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` — sibling discipline for the team-lead synthesis-pre-commit moment (different lifecycle phase).
- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` — claim-marking discipline; closure-edit commit messages should use `[VERIFIED]` markers per evidence-policy.
- `.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md` — different concept (adoption-decision convergence, not closed-loop iteration or post-audit drift closure).

- An audit fires for a drift class but the closure pattern requires non-surgical work (e.g. structural rewrite of a CLAUDE.md paragraph) — surface the bloat-vs-discipline tension.
- Cross-session bundling occurs again despite §How to apply step 4 — promote step 4 to a stronger guard or codify the explicit `git stash` workflow.

Claim 1: **VERIFIED-OPERATIONAL**, with two hygiene caveats.

Both eval scaffolds exist and cite upstreams. The promptfoo scaffold cites official promptfoo configuration docs and records `promptfoo@0.121.11` in [evals/promptfooconfig.yaml](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/promptfooconfig.yaml:2>) and [evals/promptfooconfig.yaml](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/promptfooconfig.yaml:21>). The installed npm package metadata also shows `promptfoo` version `0.121.11` in `C:\Users\42\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules\promptfoo\package.json:5`. The config shape matches promptfoo’s documented `providers`, `prompts`, `tests`, `assert`, `outputPath`, cache, and viewer model: promptfoo docs define those config fields and `promptfoo view`/web viewer behavior in the official configuration and web UI docs: https://www.promptfoo.dev/docs/configuration/reference/ and https://www.promptfoo.dev/docs/usage/web-ui/.

The DeepEval scaffold exists, cites `confident-ai/deepeval @ v4.0.0`, and the installed package metadata shows exact `Version: 4.0.0` in `Z:\venvs\claude\Lib\site-packages\deepeval-4.0.0.dist-info\METADATA:3`. The test imports DeepEval and asserts a 4.x install in [evals/deepeval/test_smoke.py](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/deepeval/test_smoke.py:56>). This aligns with DeepEval’s official positioning around metrics, LLM-as-judge scoring, 0-1 metric scores, and thresholded pass/fail semantics: https://deepeval.com/docs/metrics-introduction.

Caveats: [evals/README.md](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/README.md:19>) still says `npm install -g promptfoo@latest`, which is stale next to the pinned `0.121.11` evidence. Also [evals/deepeval/test_smoke.py](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/deepeval/test_smoke.py:32>) defaults the live LLM-as-judge test to run unless `DEEPEVAL_SKIP_LIVE=1`; the README’s “accidental pytest doesn’t burn tokens” statement at [evals/deepeval/README.md](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/deepeval/README.md:34>) is therefore misleading.

Claim 2: **PARTIAL**.

The JSONL audit trails are active, recent, and parseable as event streams. `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` has **1541** entries, last entry at [codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/state/codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl:1541>) with `ts=2026-05-10T22:28:26Z`. `codex_prepush_reviews.jsonl` has **1279** entries, last entry at [codex_prepush_reviews.jsonl](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/state/codex_prepush_reviews.jsonl:1279>) with `ts=2026-05-10T22:21:41Z`. That is within 30 days of today, May 10, 2026.

But the requested schema is not present in those JSONL streams. The last 5 postcommit records are status events: `filter_rejected_not_commit x2`, `main_entered x2`, `launched x1`. The last 5 prepush records are `filter_rejected_not_push x3`, `main_entered x2`. There is no `verdict` distribution in the JSONL records themselves, and `confidence` does not appear in either JSONL file. The real verdict lives in per-commit `codex_review_HEAD_*.txt` files, for example [codex_review_HEAD_a3d6f661.txt](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_a3d6f661.txt:1>) has `{"verdict":"approve",...}`. The vendored schema requires `verdict`, `summary`, `findings`, and `next_steps`, while `confidence` is only required inside individual findings, not as a top-level field: [review-output.schema.json](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/schemas/review-output.schema.json:6>) and [review-output.schema.json](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/schemas/review-output.schema.json:36>).

Claim 3: **SOTA-WITH-GAPS**.

Installed/configured primitives I can verify: `promptfoo`, `deepeval`, `codex-jsonl`, per-commit `codex_review_HEAD_*` verdict files, `ccusage`, RTK cost/economics surface, Langfuse Python SDK `4.2.0`, `langfuse-cli` `0.0.8`, Phoenix MCP config at [`.mcp.json`](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.mcp.json:83>), and Claude Code OTel trace export env to Phoenix at [`.claude/settings.json`](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/settings.json:36>). Phoenix is configured as the local trace sink via OTLP gRPC `127.0.0.1:14317` and UI `:16006`, with the settings comment explicitly noting that Langfuse parallel sink is deferred and logs/metrics are deferred at [`.claude/settings.json`](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/settings.json:42>).

Live `pip list` and `docker ps` executable probes were blocked by policy, so container liveness remains **not live-verified** in this audit. I used package metadata and config files as fallback evidence.

SOTA comparison sources:
- OpenLIT is an OpenTelemetry-native LLM observability platform with tracing, token/cost monitoring, eval scoring, and broad instrumentation: https://docs.openlit.io/latest/sdk/overview.
- Phoenix provides AI observability/evaluation, OTLP traces, OpenInference instrumentation, trace scoring, prompt iteration, datasets, and experiments: https://arize.com/docs/phoenix.
- Langfuse provides traces, cost/latency dashboards, prompt management, evals, datasets, and OpenTelemetry-based integrations: https://langfuse.com/docs.
- Ragas provides dedicated RAG/agent/tool metrics and evaluation APIs: https://docs.ragas.io/en/latest/references/evaluate/.
- Helicone provides gateway-level LLM cost tracking and analytics: https://docs.helicone.ai/guides/cookbooks/cost-tracking.

**Missing / Gaps**

P0:
- `evolve_pass_rate_gate.py` / automated eval pass-rate gate is missing. I found no matching file, and [evals/README.md](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/README.md:40>) explicitly says promotion to mandatory gate is future work after eval discipline matures. This means promptfoo/DeepEval are scaffolds, not regression-blocking gates.

P1:
- Normalized Codex verdict JSONL is missing. The audit JSONL exists, but it is hook telemetry, not a verdict stream with `timestamp`, `sha`, `verdict`, `confidence`.
- Langfuse trace/eval sink is not wired despite SDK/CLI being installed. Settings explicitly defer Langfuse parallel sink pending credentials or OTel Collector fan-out.
- Persistent eval-result dashboard/trend store is not operationally closed. `promptfoo view` is available locally, but DeepEval Confident dashboard and Langfuse eval dashboards are not wired.
- Phoenix/observability live liveness is not proven in this audit because `docker ps` was blocked; config is strong, live proof is missing.
- DeepEval live test cost guard is inverted relative to docs: live eval runs by default unless `DEEPEVAL_SKIP_LIVE=1`.

P2:
- OpenLIT is not installed, but Phoenix + Dash0 + ccusage/RTK cover most of the same observability/cost surface differently; OpenLIT would be a duplicate unless a single OpenTelemetry-native AI engineering platform is explicitly desired.
- Ragas is not installed; DeepEval covers general RAG metrics, so Ragas is a specialization gap rather than load-bearing today.
- Helicone is not installed; ccusage/CPA/Phoenix cover local spend/trace needs, but Helicone remains useful if gateway-level per-request SaaS-style analytics becomes a goal.
- Documentation drift: `promptfoo@latest` remains in [evals/README.md](<Z:/claude-sota-installed/evals/README.md:19>) despite installed/scaffold evidence being pinned to `0.121.11`.

Recommended install/closure priority:
1. Add a small pass-rate gate first: parse promptfoo/DeepEval results and fail on pass-rate regression before adding new eval frameworks.
2. Normalize Codex review outputs into a verdict JSONL summary stream with `{ts, sha, verdict, confidence_or_null, source_file}`.
3. Wire either Langfuse sink or an OTel Collector fan-out from the existing Phoenix trace stream.
4. Fix DeepEval live-test default so `pytest evals/` is token-safe by default.
5. Defer OpenLIT/Ragas/Helicone until there is concrete demand not covered by Phoenix + DeepEval + promptfoo + ccusage.
